| **DEC-025** | **Sharing converted QET elements.** Does sharing converted QET elements inside a company count as redistribution, and what may EW allow?<br>*Options:* Allow company-library export only with QET's licence file and a warning; never community packs; ask the QET maintainers | Allow the company-library export only with QET's licence file and a warning, never publish converted elements in community packs, and ask counsel and the QET maintainers (PQ-34). | Counsel | **G-M3**: Before the converted-element export ships (WP-S3.10 to S3.12, month 40, Feb 2030); G-M3 | The conversion feature ships without export, or ships with an unclear licence position. | 07 OQ11; PQ-34, PQ-46 |
| **DEC-026** | **Plugin signing and registry.** How are plugins signed and who runs the registry?<br>*Options:* Ed25519 with trust on first use; Sigstore countersignatures; steward-run registry with two reviewers and revocation lists | Ed25519 at 1.x; the steward runs the registry with two reviewers and revocation lists shipped with updates; decide Sigstore when the registry launches. | Project owner (steward) | **1.x**: Plugin launch (WP-S1.33, 1.x) | No plugin host can ship; community plugins have no trust path. | 17 OQ11; 19 OQ11 |
| **DEC-027** | **Plugin runtime rules.** Which runtime rules apply to plugins: hardened-runtime macOS, online rules, plugin-defined record kinds?<br>*Options:* Portable interpreter vs JIT entitlement on macOS; allow online plugin rules with budgets; defer namespaced record kinds | Measure wasmtime's portable interpreter on the sample importer and rule plugins first and add the JIT entitlement only if a rule scope misses the online budget; allow online plugin rules with fuel and epoch budgets and demotion, default plugin packs to offline and release; defer plugin record kinds behind an ADR. | Core team | **1.x**: Start of WP-S1.33 (1.x) | A weaker hardened runtime for every macOS user, or plugins that stall the editor. | 17 OQ9, OQ10, OQ19; 18 OQ13 |

Time two reference-size projects in QET during M0-M1 as the SC-3 baseline. | Pilot user (data); Core team (bounds) | **G-M0**: month 3, Jan 2027 (CP-03), before the spike | The performance gate tests the wrong fixtures; budgets are tuned to sizes the pilot never reaches or exceeds. | 00 OQ4, OQ5; 02c OQ1; PQ-01, PQ-42 |

| **DEC-045** | **QET-grid page conversion priority.** Is converting QET-grid pages to the native grid raised from Could/1.x (FR-PAG-022) to Should/1.0, and what is the "grid for new pages" default in the pilot basic project?<br>*Options:* Keep Could/1.x; raise to Should/1.0 if the pilot mostly extends migrated projects; new-page grid native (default) or QET 10 px | Raise FR-PAG-022 only if the pilot mostly extends migrated projects; ship the basic-project setting "grid for new pages", default native, pilot decides. | Pilot user | **G-M3**: Before WP-S3.10 (month 40, Feb 2030); with the lever (b) decision | Neither 4 mm nor 2.5 mm is commensurable with QET's 127/48 mm, so native symbols cannot be placed on migrated pages until conversion exists. | ADR-0027 (QET-grid consequence); 06 OQ6; PQ-06 |
| **DEC-046** | **Tag conventions and letter-code leniency.** Which tag conventions hold for the pilot basic project: page-based numbering as a house rule, fixed tags for template devices, canonical location spelling, and the letter-code leniency?<br>*Options:* Page-based -101F1 versus fixed S51/T55; "+J-E-2416" (atomic node code) versus "+JE2416"; ADR-0026 mapping accepting S for S51 and S56 (strict 2009 letters would be Q and B); severity of EW-RDS-003 | Keep "+J-E-2416" with an info finding in the drafting scheme, accept S as the shipped leniency (only T55 reported, golden U-37) recorded in the ew-rds mapping with verified=false, warning for frozen and new tags with a frozen_severity parameter. | Pilot user | **G-M1**: Before the goldens freeze (WP-S7.01, month 13, Nov 2027); G-M1 | Goldens for numbering, check findings and letter-code conformance are frozen on assumptions and re-baselined later. | 02a OQ2; ADR-0026 (leniency note); PQ-08 |
| **DEC-047** | **Strip and terminal conventions.** How are -X1031 and -X1032 modelled: one PT 2.5-TWIN or one level per drawn terminal, "1+" as designation or as printed function text, and which per-class numbering rule applies?<br>*Options:* Sequential 1-20 with "1+" as function text and 20+5 TWIN; designations "1+", "1-", "PE"; 10+5 TWIN; numbering rule per device class in the pilot basic project | Adopt the golden proposal (sequential designations, function text, 20+5) and per-class numbering rules for strips, and confirm with the pilot before the terminal-diagram goldens freeze. | Pilot user | **G-M1**: Before the terminal-diagram goldens freeze (WP-S7.01, month 13, Nov 2027; WP-S3.03, month 10, Aug 2027); G-M1 | Terminal diagrams (903 to 905) and the strip BOM are frozen on a guess. | 02a OQ5; 03 OQ8; PQ-10 |
| **DEC-048** | **Text height, technical data, -XPE / -UC1.** Which minimum text height, technical-data notation and main-PE tagging (-XPE, -UC1) apply?<br>*Options:* Text-height scheme parameter 1.2 mm or 1.8 mm; "C10" versus "10A C"; per-class numbering and waiver W-1 for -XPE's missing part | Scheme parameter 1.2 mm in the golden scheme, per-device-class numbering rules and waiver W-1 until the pilot answers. | Pilot user | **G-M1**: Before WP-S7.01 (month 13, Nov 2027) and FR-CHK-023 (WP-S1.16, month 27, Jan 2029); G-M1 | The house style's 6.66 pt texts are flagged, or frozen tags change. | 20 OQ5; PQ-36 |
| **DEC-049** | **Wire numbering and marking default.** What are the default wire-numbering and marking schemes of the pilot basic project?<br>*Options:* Potential-oriented for control circuits and connection-oriented for mains; off until the pilot chooses; DIN versus destination marking | Ship potential-oriented numbering for control circuits and connection-oriented for mains, off by default until the pilot chooses, decided together with the manufacturing marking scheme (see shop policies). | Pilot user | **G-M1**: Golden numbers WP-S7.01 (month 13, Nov 2027); pilot basic project WP-S7.03 (month 36, Oct 2029) | The reference set has no wire numbers (D-09), so the goldens carry invented ones and the basic project ships without a default. | 02a OQ10; 08 (golden wire numbers, U-33); PQ-22 |
| **DEC-050** | **Cross-reference and contact-image style.** Which cross-reference and contact-image style do native projects and the pilot basic project use?<br>*Options:* "/101.H16" EPLAN grid style on native frames; QET style (%F-%l%c) kept for the pilot basic project; contact images "in path" with "13-14 /105.A3"; interruption-point direction from the edge or from the potential's source | EPLAN style on native frames, QET style in the pilot basic project and migrated projects, contact images in path for native projects, edge heuristic for IP direction with a source-based option, revisited after the QET corpus import. | Pilot user | **G-M2**: WP-S7.03 (month 36, Oct 2029); first look at R0 (month 26, Dec 2028) | The pilot's drawings look different from their QET output, or the wrong default is learned in R1. | 02a OQ11; 06 OQ9, OQ10; PQ-51 |
| **DEC-051** | **Colour profile and PE practice.** Which conductor colour profile and PE practice are the defaults?<br>*Options:* Keep DBU/WH for 24 V DC or offer EN 60204-1 BU / IEC 60445 RD/WH; exact DBU print colour; PE wire size and colour; 0 V to PE bond; earthing system TN-S, TN-C-S or TT; incoming supply cable; whether L+ counts as associated line conductor | Keep the pilot's DBU/WH profile, ship the unverified entries with verified=false, default line_types L, L1-L3; set SupplySpec.earthing from the pilot's answer. | Pilot user | **G-M1**: Before the goldens freeze (WP-S7.01, month 13, Nov 2027); G-M1 | D-19, D-37 and D-38 tests assert wrong values; PE sizing results differ on DC devices. | 08 OQ8, OQ9; PQ-05, PQ-17 |

| **DEC-053** | **Title-block Customer binding.** Does the title-block "Customer" show the contracting party or the end user?<br>*Options:* Contracting party; end user; configurable binding per scheme | Bind to a Party role chosen in the basic project; default to the contracting party unless the pilot says otherwise. | Pilot user | **G-M1**: Before the goldens freeze (WP-S3.02, month 7, May 2027; WP-S7.01, month 13, Nov 2027) | Defect D-30 test and title-block goldens change after freeze. | PQ-11 |
| **DEC-054** | **Report folio layout.** Are reports folios 903-911 in section 9 or separate documents, one terminal-diagram block per strip or one grouped block, and is folio 901 in component or assembly mode?<br>*Options:* Folios in section 9 versus separate documents; separate blocks (Golden A 903/904/905) versus grouped block with NewFolio; component mode versus assembly mode (purchasing export) | Folios in section 9; separate blocks in the pilot basic project with a grouped NewFolio option and coverage check EW-CORE-026; component mode for folio 901, assembly mode as purchasing export. | Pilot user | **G-M1**: WP-S3.03 (month 10, Aug 2027); G-M1 | The report engine defaults (WP-S3.03, S3.06) are reworked after the golden PDFs exist. | 09 OQ3; 20 OQ9; PQ-12 |
| **DEC-055** | **Revision workflow and checker.** What is the pilot's revision cycle, and who can check when an engineer draws alone?<br>*Options:* Approval and as-built steps; index format; what changed in V2.0 on folios 101 and 106; hard four-eyes rule versus a bypass; DRAFT status mark auto or off; git commit at release | Keep the hard rule (unchecked deliveries go out as exports of the open revision, DRAFT mark from 1.0); status mark auto in the shipped basic project and off in the pilot basic project; ask who can act as checker. | Pilot user | **G-M2**: Before WP-S1.18 (month 25, Nov 2028); G-M2 | The revision model and release gate are built for a process that is not the pilot's; solo engineers cannot release. | 15 OQ8, OQ9; 05 OQ7; PQ-09 |

| **DEC-058** | **Project languages and bilingual output.** Which project languages, bilingual output, and company dictionary does the pilot need?<br>*Options:* NL, EN, DE, FR; one PDF per language (default) versus stacked bilingual pages | Support both from 1.0 with one PDF per language as the default; stacked text is a form filter, not a code path; company dictionary in the basic project. | Pilot user | **G-M2**: WP-S7.03 (month 36, Oct 2029); bilingual output before WP-S3.15 (month 56, Jun 2031) | MlText defaults in the basic project and the translation workflow are guessed. | 09 OQ14; PQ-28 |

### 3.3 Low-impact defaults to confirm as one batch

DEC-080 confirms these in a single core-team review. Each row has a default that the documents already
use; a row is struck or changed only if someone objects. Items 1 to 4 were confirmed early, on 2026-10-18 (§2.4
ID-3).

| # | Default | Confirm by | Sources |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Keep 256 fixed shards even though small projects then have about 80 shard files. | **Confirmed 2026-10-18** (project owner (delegated, 2026-10-18)); was due at G-M0, before the format spec is written (WP-S1.09, month 4, Feb 2027) | 05 OQ13 |
| 2 | No macOS package bundle for `.ew` in v1; reconsider at 1.x if macOS pilot users ask. | **Confirmed 2026-10-18** (project owner (delegated, 2026-10-18)) | 05 OQ14 |
| 3 | The manifest stays tracked in git; the trivial `generation` conflict of merges made without the driver is tolerated. | **Confirmed 2026-10-18** (project owner (delegated, 2026-10-18)) | 05 OQ6 |
| 4 | Duplicate requirement rows FR-REV-018/FR-COL-018 and FR-REV-019/FR-COL-019: keep the REV rows, the COL rows refer to them and keep their IDs. | **Confirmed 2026-10-18** (project owner (delegated, 2026-10-18)); catalogue edit applied: FR-REV-018/019 absorb the COL rows' detail, FR-COL-018/019 refer to them | 15 OQ11 |
| 5 | Golden B keeps letters (`TagSpec.letter`); letter changes are tested only by B-prime (identifier-set switch). | G-M1 (WP-S7.01, month 13, Nov 2027) | 20 OQ4 |
| 6 | BU cores of vendor cables on 0 V (EF-11): keep the warning, no exemption, part-scoped waiver from 1.0. | G-M1 (WP-S1.16 rule text, month 27, Jan 2029) | 08 OQ11; 15 OQ6; 20 OQ6 |
| 7 | Released-number tombstones are read from the `FreezeNumbers` committed computation, not from the archive; confirm with the numbering design of 06. | WP-S1.11 (month 20, Jun 2028); G-M2 | 15 OQ17 |
| 8 | Router: benchmark sequential PathFinder on the L fixture at M4 and switch to the iteration-parallel variant only if NFR-PERF-16 is missed. | G-M4 (WP-S4.07, month 51, Jan 2031) | 12 OQ11 |
| 9 | SARIF placement: emit the record's project file and line as `physicalLocation` plus page and entity as logical locations; verify the rendering on a sample repository before 1.0. | G-M5 | 15 OQ19 |

### 3.4 Questions settled elsewhere

These questions still read as open in their own document, but another document or the plan has settled them. They
need no decision; the documents should be corrected when next edited.

| Question | Settled in |

## 4. Part C: questions for the pilot user

### 4.1 How to read it

The list merges PQ-01 to PQ-36 of the [brainstorm](../design/01-brainstorm.md#7-questions-for-the-pilot-user) with
17 questions that the consolidation added (PQ-37 to PQ-53, marked *new*). The brainstorm should adopt the new IDs
(open question 1). Priority is the moment by which the question must be **asked**, because the answer is needed
later, and some answers need weeks:

| Priority | Ask by | Why |
|---|---|---|
| **P1** | The kick-off (WP-S0.08, Nov 2026) | Needed by G-M0 (month 3, Jan 2027), overdue (LG-5), or a long lead time (data, legal) |
| **P2** | Month 6 (Apr 2027) | The answer freezes a golden, a default or the spine (WP-S7.01, WP-S3.03, WP-S3.04 around month 10, Aug 2027 to month 15, Jan 2028) |
| **P3** | Month 16 (Feb 2028, G-M1) | Needed for MVP design and the basic project (WP-S1.17 to WP-S1.21, WP-S4.01, WP-S7.03) |
| **P4** | Month 37 (Nov 2029) | Needed for the 1.0 features (PLC exchange, manufacturing, 3D) |

Until a question is answered, the documented default in the third column applies; the golden project marks such
values with a triangle. Some questions (the first topic) go to the employer through the pilot user.

### 4.4 Recording the answers

An answer is recorded in the decision record (§5) as "Answered *date* by pilot user" with the value; a person's name
never goes into the repository. If the answer changes a golden or a default, the change is a reviewed edit of the
golden project or the basic project, and the P-number of the question is quoted in the commit message. Confidential
material the pilot supplies (the `.qet` files, part lists, the reference PDF) stays outside the repository and is used
only in private CI and in private review sessions (DEC-073, DEC-059).

---

## 5. Maintaining the log

- **Closing a decision.** The owner decides; the decision is written into the gate record (template in
  [gates §7](03-gates.md#7-gate-review-template)), or into an ADR if it is architectural or legal (Proposed to
  Accepted). Then the affected requirement rows, work packages and documents are edited, and the row moves from
  Part B to Part A with the date and the reference. The row is not deleted from history: the commit that moves it
  carries the reasoning.
- **Opening a decision.** A question that no document can answer gets a DEC row with the next free number, the same
  columns, and a gate. A new Proposed ADR gets a row at the same time.
- **Rhythm.** The project lead reviews the rows due at the next gate in the monthly plan review
  ([global plan §11.2](01-global-plan.md#112-tracking-and-re-planning)); the gate pack lists the decisions "taken or
  due" (gates §7, section 6).
- **After G-M2** no ADR remains Proposed (gates §4.3). A decision that is still open after its gate becomes a risk
  item in the next gate pack.
- **Never recorded here:** names of people, customers or the panel builder, project or drawing identifiers, the
  confidentiality notice, e-mail addresses and local paths ([20](../design/20-golden-project.md) §3.5).
- **Interim defaults.** When work must proceed before the owner of a row decides, the project owner may put the
  row's recommendation in force as the working basis. The row stays open, its recommendation cell says "Default in
  force since *date*", and §2.4 lists it, so this log remains the single register.

## Open questions

1. **New pilot questions in the brainstorm.** PQ-37 to PQ-52 are now in the brainstorm's §7 table (resolved).
   PQ-53 (the preview scope and the Must-row triage) is new in version 0.2 and exists only in this log.
   *Recommendation:* the brainstorm editor appends it to its §7 table with this ID.
2. **Gate of the PLC bit order.** DEC-061 is gated G-M2 (WP-S1.21), although the golden test input H1 is already
   used at G-M1. *Recommendation:* ask at the kick-off anyway; the plan does not wait for the answer.
3. **Employer questions through the pilot user.** PQ-31, PQ-37, PQ-38 and PQ-39 need the employer, not the engineer.
   *Recommendation:* the project owner asks the employer directly and the pilot user is informed.
4. **Month labels.** The gate months of §1.3 are a generated block; the WP months in the register are copied from
   [wp-schedule.csv](wp-schedule.csv) (start month, rounded up), which `schedule.py` rewrites, but no script rewrites
   the text of this log. *Recommendation:* regenerate the WP months by hand from the CSV when the plan is re-based at
   G-M1 (PD-11); until then the gate, not the month, is binding.
5. **Counsel capacity.** Five rows (DEC-015, DEC-017, DEC-020, DEC-025, DEC-083) depend on
   counsel and are listed with the gates by which the answer is needed, but the engagement itself is not yet
   scheduled. *Recommendation:* commission counsel in the first 90 days (global plan §15).
6. **Governance timing for channels, donations and NLnet.** Governance §3.7 and §3.9 still open Discussions, chat and
   donations at the first binary release (now the MVP at month 41) and scope a single first NLnet proposal to the
   format crates, importers and `ew check` at M1. The global plan (§13.2, §13.3, OQ3, OQ7) opens Discussions at M1 and
   donations when the steward holds the organisation, and splits the funding into three proposals: the first at the
   first call after LG-1 is signed (target month 3), the second at G-M1, the third at G-M2. *Recommendation:* take
   DEC-084 and DEC-005 as written and let the governance editor amend §3.7 and §3.9.
7. **Which dates may leave the team.** DEC-081 keeps the AI-moderate dates internal. *Recommendation:* the project
   lead checks the monthly progress note and `ROADMAP.md` for internal dates before each publication until G-M1.
8. **Left open by the fourth implementation cycle** (v0.5). (a) The system library's identity: `ew-import-qet`
   keeps the provisional constant `BUILTIN_LIBRARY` until WP-S1.19a names it ([07](../design/07-symbols-parts-macros.md)
   open question 17). (b) How mirrored texts flip their alignment in `ew-text`. (c) Raw model reads in crates that
   depend on `ew-derive`: cargo features are additive, so those crates compile with the `raw-read` API present
   (ADR-0001 amendment 1). *Recommendation:* (a) with WP-S1.19a before format 0.1; (b) with the text work of
   `ew-text`; (c) a per-crate clippy `disallowed-methods` list for the raw API, decided with the code lead.
   *Status (v0.7):* (a) still open, and the starter content now uses the same provisional UUID (ID-33); (b) closed:
   the project owner decided the rule for all eight variants (ID-49, [07](../design/07-symbols-parts-macros.md)
   §4.1); (c) decided as `cargo xtask check-raw-read`, implementation tracked as FT-01 (ID-50). *Status (v0.8):* (c) implemented in cycle 6 (ID-77), with call detection moving to a generated clippy list (ID-94, FT-32).
